# 11 Peripherals interconnect matrix

## 11.1 Introduction

Several peripherals have direct connections between them.

This allows autonomous communication and or synchronization between peripherals, saving CPU resources thus power supply consumption.

In addition, these hardware connections remove software latency and allow design of predictable system.

Depending on peripherals, these interconnections can operate in Run, Sleep, Low-power run and sleep, Stop 0 and Stop 1 modes.

The HRTIM burst operation can be triggered by on chip event coming from other general purpose timer.

The burst mode controller counter can be clocked by general purpose timers as well as shown in the [Table 64](#XREF_73863__Table_33__Table_Interconnect_12%1143).

 


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableCap"><h4 class="TableCap" id="2576327">Table 64. <a id="12%1143" name="XREF_73863__Table_33__Table_Interconnect_12%1143">Interconnect 12</a></h4></caption><col width="269.29px"/><col width="269.29px"/><thead><tr height="42.67px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="2576331" name="360_TableHeadC_2576331"></a>HRTIM Burst mode trigger event/ clock signal</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">HRTIM Burst mode trigger event/ clock signal assignment</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_bm_trg</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim7_trgo</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_bm_ck1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim16_oc</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_bm_ck2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim17_oc</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_bm_ck3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim7_trgo‘</p></td></tr></table></div>


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableCap"><h4 class="TableCap" id="2576596">Table 65. <a id="13%1143" name="XREF_42625__Table_34__Interconnect_13%1143">Interconnect 13</a></h4></caption><col width="269.29px"/><col width="269.29px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="2576600" name="360_TableHeadC_2576600"></a>HRTIM update enable signal</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">HRTIM update enable assignment</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_upd_en1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim16_oc</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_upd_en2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim17_oc</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_upd_en3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim6_oc</p></td></tr></table></div>


The HRTIM can be synchronized by external sources as shown in the [Table 66](#XREF_95438__Table_35__Interconnect_14%1143).


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableCap"><h4 class="TableCap" id="2576645">Table 66. <a id="14%1143" name="XREF_95438__Table_35__Interconnect_14%1143">Interconnect 14</a></h4></caption><col width="269.29px"/><col width="269.29px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="2576649" name="360_TableHeadC_2576649"></a>HRTIM synchronization signals</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">HRTIM synchronization signal assignment</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_in_sync2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">tim1_trgo</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyL">hrtim_in_sync3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">HRTIM_SCIN</p></td></tr></table></div>


Some of the TIMx timers are linked together internally for timer synchronization or chaining.

When one timer is configured in Master Mode, it can reset, start, stop or clock the counter of another timer configured in Slave Mode.

A description of the feature is provided in: [Section 29.3.30: Timer synchronization](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_50306__12_4_19_TIM1_and_external_trigger_synchronization21).

The modes of synchronization are detailed in:

- [Section 29.3.30: Timer synchronization](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_50306__12_4_19_TIM1_and_external_trigger_synchronization21) for advanced-control timers (TIM1/TIM8/TIM20)
- [Section 30.4.23: Timer synchronization](General-purpose_timers_%28TIM2__47_TIM3__47_TIM4__47_TIM5%29.md#XREF_17049__12_4_14_Timer_synchronization41) for general-purpose timers (TIM2/TIM3/TIM4/TIM5)
- [Section 31.4.26: Timer synchronization (TIM15 only)](General_purpose_timers_%28TIM15__47_TIM16__47_TIM17%29.md#XREF_90212__32_4_21_Timer_synchronization_(TIM15)22) for general-purpose timer (TIM15)

#### Triggering signals

The output (from Master) is on signal TIMx\_TRGO (and TIMx\_TRGO2 for TIM1/TIM8/TIM20) following a configurable timer event.

The input (to slave) is on signals TIMx\_ITRx

The input and output signals for TIM1/TIM8/TIM20 are shown in [Figure 296: Advanced-control timer block diagram](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_53652__Figure_26__Advanced_control_timer_block_diagram21).

The possible master/slave connections are given in:

- [Table 267: Internal trigger connection](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_52337__Table_248__TIMx_internal_trigger_connection21)

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

### 11.3.2 From timer (TIMx, HRTIM) and EXTI to ADC (ADCx)

Timers (TIMx, HRTIM) can be used to generate an ADC triggering event.

TIMx synchronization is described in: [Section 29.3.31: ADC triggers](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_19651__1_4_18_Timer_synchronization21) (TIM1/TIM8).

ADC synchronization is described in: [Section 21.4.18: Conversion on external trigger and trigger polarity (EXTSEL, EXTEN, JEXTSEL, JEXTEN)](Analog-to-digital_converters_%28ADC%29.md#XREF_37535__1_5_Conversion_on_external_trigger_and_trigger_polarity_(EXTSEL,10).

#### Triggering signals

The output (from timer) is on signal TIMx\_TRGO, TIMx\_TRGO2 or TIMx\_CCx event.

The input (to ADC) is on signal EXT[15:0], JEXT[15:0].

The connection between timers and ADC is provided in:

- [Table 166: ADC1/2 - External triggers for regular channels](Analog-to-digital_converters_%28ADC%29.md#XREF_41663__Table_78__ADC1_2_-_External_triggers_for_regular_channels10)
- [Table 167: ADC1/2 - External trigger for injected channels](Analog-to-digital_converters_%28ADC%29.md#XREF_82634__Table_137__ADC1_2_-_External_trigger_for_injected_channels10)

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

### 11.3.3 From ADC (ADCx) to timer (TIMx, HRTIM)

See please [Table 75: Interconnect 2](#XREF_97119__Table_44__Interconnect_2_(continued)43) and [Table 83.: Interconnect 10](#XREF_37203__Table_52__Interconnect_10_(continued)43)

ADCs Analog watchdogs are connected to TIM1/8/20 for digital power applications (cycle-bycycle current regulation with ADC).

A description of the ADC analog watchdog setting is provided in: [Section 21.4.28: Analog window watchdog (AWD1EN, JAWD1EN, AWD1SGL, AWD1CH, AWD2CH, AWD3CH, AWD\_HTx, AWD\_LTx, AWDx)](Analog-to-digital_converters_%28ADC%29.md#XREF_35523__1_7_Analog_window_watchdog_(AWDxEN,_JAWDxEN,_AWDxSGL,10).

Trigger settings on the timer are provided in: [Section 29.3.6: External trigger input](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_15145__16_3_4_External_trigger_input21).

#### Triggering signals

The output (from ADC) is on signals ADCn\_AWDx\_OUT and the input (to timer) on signal TIMx\_ETR (external trigger) or hrtim\_eevx[4:1].

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

Timers (TIMx, HRTIM) and EXTI can be used as triggering event to start a DAC conversion.

#### Triggering signals

The output (from timer) is on signal TIMx\_TRGO directly connected to corresponding DAC inputs.

Selection of input triggers on DAC is provided in [Section 22.4.7: DAC trigger selection](Digital-to-analog_converter_%28DAC%29.md#XREF_33173__17_3_6_DAC_trigger_selection9) (single and dual mode).

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

### 11.3.5 From HSE, LSE, LSI, HSI16, MCO, RTC to timer (TIMx)

See please [Table 77: Interconnect 4](#XREF_17303__Table_46__Interconnect_4_(continued)43) and [Table 75: Interconnect 2](#XREF_97119__Table_44__Interconnect_2_(continued)43)

External clocks (HSE, LSE), internal clocks (LSI, HSI16), microcontroller output clock (MCO), GPIO and RTC wakeup interrupt can be used as input to timer (TIMx).

This allows to calibrate the HSI16 and precisely measure the LSI oscillator frequency.

When Low Speed External (LSE) oscillator is used, no additional hardware connections are required.

This feature is described in [Section 7.2.16: Internal/external clock measurement with TIM5/TIM15/TIM16/TIM17](Reset_and_clock_control_%28RCC%29.md#XREF_11631__7_2_12_Internal_external_clock_measurement_with_TIM1615).

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

RTC alarm A/B, RTC\_TAMP1/2/3 input detection, COMPx\_OUT can be used as trigger to start LPTIM counters (LPTIM1).

#### Triggering signals

This trigger feature is described in [Section 33.4.6: Trigger multiplexer](Low-power_timer_%28LPTIM%29.md#XREF_63409__30_4_5_Trigger_multiplexer33) (and following sections).

The input selection is described in [Table 327: LPTIM1 external trigger connection](Low-power_timer_%28LPTIM%29.md#XREF_12555__Table_211__LPTIM1_external_trigger_connection33).

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep, Stop 0, Stop 1.

Timers (TIMx) can be used as blanking window to COMPx (x = 1...7)

The blanking function is described in [Section 24.3.6: COMP output blanking](Comparator_%28COMP%29.md#XREF_26128__21_3_6_COMP_output_blanking27).

The blanking sources are given in comparators control and status register (COMP\_CxCSR) bits BLANKSEL[2:0].

#### Triggering signals

Timer output signal TIMx\_Ocx are the inputs to blanking source of COMPx.

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

### 11.3.9 From comparators (COMPx) to timers (TIMx, HRTIM)

Comparators (COMPx) output values can be connected to:

- Timers (TIMx) input captures or TIMx\_ETR signals or TIMx\_OCREFCLR signals.
- hrtim\_eevx[4:1] and hrtim\_in\_fltx[4:1]

Comparators (COMPx) output values can also generate break input signals for timers (TIMx) see Section 30.3.17: Bidirectional break inputs.

The timer break input features two channels:

- A break channel which gathers both application fault (from input pins and built-in comparators) and system-level fault (clock failure, parity error, ...).
- A break2 channel which only includes application faults (from input pins and built-in comparators).

Refer to [Table 81](#XREF_73793__Table_50__Interconnect_8%1143), [Table 82](#XREF_72547__Table_51__Interconnect_9%1143) and [Table 85](#XREF_32692__Table_54__Interconnect_18%1143).

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

### 11.3.10 From system errors to timers (TIMx) and HRTIM

TIMx (TIM1/TIM8/TIM20/TIM15/TIM16/TIM17) break inputs and HRTIM system fault input gather MCU internal fault events coming from:

- the clock failure event generated by the clock security system (CSS),
- the PVD output,
- the SRAM parity error signal,
- the Cortex-M4 LOCKUP (Hardfault) output
- Flash ECC double error detection

The purpose of the break function is to protect power switches driven by PWM signals generated by the timers.

The functionality is described in:

- [Section 29.3.18: Using the break function](Advanced-control_timers_%28TIM1__47_TIM8__47_TIM20%29.md#XREF_33995__13_4_12_Using_the_break_function21) (TIM1/TIM8/TIM20)
- [Section 31.4.15: Using the break function](General_purpose_timers_%28TIM15__47_TIM16__47_TIM17%29.md#XREF_33995__13_4_12_Using_the_break_function22) (TIM15/TIM16/TIM17/TIM20)

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.

### 11.3.11 From timers (TIM16/TIM17) to IRTIM

 


<div align="center"><table class="TableTitle"><caption class="TableCap"><h4 class="TableCap" id="2597879">Table 86. <a id="16%1143" name="XREF_41158__Table_55__Interconnect_16%1143">Interconnect 16</a></h4></caption><col width="269.29px"/><col width="269.29px"/><thead><tr height="28.00px"><th><p class="TableHeadC"><a id="2674367" name="360_TableHeadC_2674367"></a>IRTIM control signal</p></th><th><p class="TableHeadC">IRTIM control signals assignment</p></th></tr></thead><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">modulation envelope signal</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">tim16_oc1</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="TableBodyC">carrier signal</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyC">tim17_oc1</p></td></tr></table></div>


General-purpose timer (TIM16/TIM17) output channel TIMx\_OC1 are used to generate the waveform of infrared signal output.

The functionality is described in [Section 34: Infrared interface (IRTIM)](Infrared_interface_%28IRTIM%29.md#XREF_32630__21_Infra-red_remote_control_function_(IR)44).

#### Active power mode

Run, Sleep, Low-power run, Low-power sleep.
